+package com.bentza.sna.audit;
+
+import static org.junit.jupiter.api.Assertions.assertArrayEquals;
+import static org.junit.jupiter.api.Assertions.assertEquals;
+import static org.junit.jupiter.api.Assertions.assertTrue;
+
+import java.io.File;
+import java.nio.charset.StandardCharsets;
+import java.nio.file.Files;
+import java.util.Arrays;
+import java.util.List;
+import java.util.TreeSet;
+import java.util.stream.Collectors;
+
+import org.junit.jupiter.api.Test;
+
+/**
+ * 2.2.2: installers ship exactly the sample networks that AGNA 2.2.1 shipped (the ten files embedded in the jar).
+ * The repository's samples/ folder also holds test fixtures (Chain1.txt, "4 full.txt", ...) that must never reach
+ * an installer; packaging/stage-samples.sh copies only what packaging/samples-manifest.txt lists.
+ */
+public class InstallerSamplesManifestTest
+    {
+    private static List<String> manifest() throws Exception
+        {
+        return Files.readAllLines(new File("packaging/samples-manifest.txt").toPath(), StandardCharsets.UTF_8)
+                .stream().map(String::trim).filter(s -> !s.isEmpty()).collect(Collectors.toList());
+        }
+
+    @Test
+    public void manifestIsExactlyTheSetEmbeddedInTheJar() throws Exception
+        {
+        String[] embedded = new File("agna-core/src/main/resources/samples").list();
+        assertTrue(embedded != null, "run the tests from the repository root");
+        assertEquals(new TreeSet<>(Arrays.asList(embedded)), new TreeSet<>(manifest()),
+                "installer samples must equal the sample set embedded in the jar (the 2.2.1 set)");
+        assertEquals(10, manifest().size());
+        }
+
+    @Test
+    public void manifestFilesAreByteIdenticalToTheEmbeddedOnes() throws Exception
+        {
+        for (String name : manifest())
+            {
+            byte[] shipped = Files.readAllBytes(new File("samples", name).toPath());
+            byte[] embedded = Files.readAllBytes(new File("agna-core/src/main/resources/samples", name).toPath());
+            assertArrayEquals(embedded, shipped, name);
+            }
+        }
+
+    @Test
+    public void testFixturesAreNeverListed() throws Exception
+        {
+        for (String name : manifest())
+            {
+            String n = name.toLowerCase();
+            assertTrue(!n.startsWith("chain") && !n.contains("full"), "test fixture in installer manifest: " + name);
+            }
+        }
+    }

--- a/agna-core/src/test/java/com/bentza/sna/net/ClosenessBoundTest.java	1970-01-01 00:00:00.000000000 +0000
+++ b/agna-core/src/test/java/com/bentza/sna/net/ClosenessBoundTest.java	2026-11-20 07:57:02.180636750 +0000

+package com.bentza.sna.net;
+
+import static org.junit.jupiter.api.Assertions.assertEquals;
+import static org.junit.jupiter.api.Assertions.assertTrue;
+
+import org.junit.jupiter.api.Test;
+
+/** 2.2.2: closeness stays within [0, 1] when some actors cannot reach others. */
+public class ClosenessBoundTest
+    {
+    @Test
+    public void standardisedClosenessNeverExceedsOne()
+        {
+        // 0 <-> 1 reach each other; 2 -> 3 is a separate pair; 4 is isolated
+        Network net = new Network(5);
+        net.setValue(1f, 0, 1);
+        net.setValue(1f, 1, 0);
+        net.setValue(1f, 2, 3);
+        net.setValue(1f, 3, 2);
+        float[] c = new AgnaLib().closeness(net);
+        for (int i = 0; i < 5; i++)
+            {
+            double std = c[i] * (5 - 1);
+            assertTrue(std >= 0 && std <= 1.0 + 1e-6, "actor " + i + " standardised closeness " + std);
+            }
+        // actor 0 reaches 1 of 4 others at distance 1: (1/4) * (1/1) = 0.25 (2.2.1 reported 4 * 1/1 = 4.0)
+        assertEquals(0.25, c[0] * 4, 1e-6);
+        assertEquals(0.0, c[4], 1e-9);
+        }
+
+    @Test
+    public void connectedNetworksAreUnchanged()
+        {
+        // path 0 - 1 - 2: actor 1 has fareness 2, closeness 1/2, standardised 1.0
+        Network net = new Network(3);
+        net.setValue(1f, 0, 1); net.setValue(1f, 1, 0);
+        net.setValue(1f, 1, 2); net.setValue(1f, 2, 1);
+        float[] c = new AgnaLib().closeness(net);
+        assertEquals(0.5, c[1], 1e-6);
+        assertEquals(1.0 / 3.0, c[0], 1e-6);
+        }
+    }

 ---
 
+## [2.2.2] - 2026-11-20
+
+Correction release for 2.2.1. It fixes the wrong results and the unsafe input handling found in the post-2.2.1 audit and reproduced on the published 2.2.1. No feature is added; every fix was verified against the 2.2.1 behaviour (see "How this release was verified").
+
+### Fixed: wrong results
+- **`agna metrics` (and the MCP and Python `metrics` calls): distances were computed with a stack (depth-first) instead of a queue (breadth-first).** Geodesic hop counts, eccentricity, diameter, closeness and betweenness were wrong on most networks that contain a cycle. On the bundled samples 2.2.1 was wrong for 8 of 10 networks.
+- **`agna distance FROM TO --out FILE`:** the hop count and path used the same depth-first search (Zachary: Member 15 to Member 4 gave 4 hops, the shortest is 3).
+- **Closeness could exceed its maximum of 1 when some actors could not reach others** (desktop, `agna analyse`, `agna metrics`). Closeness now uses the Wasserman-Faust normalisation (identical on strongly connected networks, always within [0, 1]).
+- **`agna metrics` no longer reports diameter and eccentricity when some actor cannot reach another** (undefined there).
+- **Louvain community detection ran only the first phase** (no aggregation), so modularity was systematically too low (Zachary karate club: Q = 0.361, now 0.416).
+- **Bonacich power centrality returned a truncated, divergent series when beta was at or above 1/lambda-max.** It is now computed exactly, the admissible range is shown, and an inadmissible beta is reported with an explanation instead of garbage. Eigenvector centrality uses a converged solver.
+
+### Clarified
+- **Betweenness counting convention is now stated next to the numbers:** raw betweenness is summed over ordered pairs, so on an undirected network it is twice the NetworkX/igraph unnormalised value. The `analyse` report has a footnote, `metrics --json` has an additive `conventions.betweenness` field, and `metrics` CSV prints a note on standard error. No value changed.
+
+- **Citation DOI:** the software, the Help page, `CITATION.cff`, `CITATION.bib` and the README now cite the all-versions Zenodo DOI 10.5281/zenodo.22708198, which always resolves to the latest release; 2.2.1 cited its own version DOI (10.5281/zenodo.24226451).
+
+### Packaging
+- Installers ship exactly the ten sample networks shipped with 2.2.1. The input folder given to `jpackage` is now staged from `packaging/samples-manifest.txt`, so test fixtures from the repository's `samples/` folder (`Chain1.txt`, `Chain2.txt`, `4 full.txt`) can no longer end up in an installer; a unit test guards the manifest.
+
+### Fixed: hangs and data loss
+- **`agna draw` (and image export) never finished for any network file without viewer settings** (`Has Viewer no`, including files written by Agna itself, and most generated networks): a grid step of 0 made the grid loop run forever. The step is now clamped; images are pixel-identical to 2.2.1 wherever 2.2.1 finished.
+- **CSV files with quoted names lost actors.** A name such as `"José ""Pepe"", Jr."` was split at its comma, so 2.2.1 loaded 3 of 4 actors and renamed them 1, 2, 3 without any warning. CSV (and, new, `.tsv`) files are now read as RFC 4180: quoted names, `;` or tab separators and a byte-order mark all work. Files that loaded before load identically.
+
+### Fixed: input handling
+- **Corrupt input was accepted as a valid network.** Random bytes, text or NaN cells, non-square or ragged CSV, Pajek arcs to missing vertices and header-less `.agn` files all loaded with exit code 0. A new integrity check refuses them (exit code 2) with a message naming the problem. `agna validate` lists every issue; `--lenient` loads a damaged file best-effort.
+- **Several `agna analyse` text reports contained only a title and no values** (clustering, transitivity, k-core, triad census, structural holes, connectivity, eigenvector, Bonacich, PageRank). They now print their tables.
+
+### Fixed: command-line contract
+- **`agna generate star --nodes 8` silently created a random network.** The network type may be given as a positional argument; an unknown type or option is an error (exit code 1) with the list of valid types.
+- **`agna matrix --format csv`:** the header row was one column short, so the output could not be re-imported. An empty corner cell is now written, and names that contain the separator are quoted.
+- **Exit codes:** 1 usage error, 2 data error (corrupt or missing file), 3 computation error. Every failure used to exit with 1.
+- **`agna metrics` printed its notes to standard output before the JSON,** which broke machine-readable output. Notes now go to standard error.
+- **`agna validate` JSON:** density is rounded (no float noise such as 0.4000000059604645); integrity issues and parser warnings are listed.
+- **MCP server:** a malformed line now gets a JSON-RPC `-32700` reply and a batch request gets `-32600`, instead of silence.
+
+### How this release was verified
+- **420 unit tests** (273 core, 80 CLI, 67 desktop), including regression tests for every fix.
+- **Differential testing** (`tools/audit/differential.py`, `classify_differences.py`): 1,365 command runs (68 commands on 21 inputs: the bundled samples plus edge-case files) with the unmodified 2.2.1 jar and the 2.2.2 jar. 957 are byte-identical; every other difference was classified by an automatic check into the fixes listed above (title-only reports now filled, closeness bounded, distances corrected, header alignment, additive validation output, Louvain, quoted-name CSV). No unexplained difference remains.
+- **NetworkX parity** (`tools/nx-parity`): 65 designed and real networks, 3,360 checks of degree, distance, centrality, clustering, k-core, eigenvector, Bonacich, PageRank and community indices. 2.2.1: 3,280 pass, 68 fail. 2.2.2: 3,353 pass, 0 fail, 5 warnings (Louvain is heuristic and may differ from the reference by a small modularity gap), 2 skipped (degenerate eigenvectors). Nothing that passed on 2.2.1 fails on 2.2.2.
+- **Robustness battery** (`tools/audit/cli_battery.py`, 667 runs on valid, edge-case and hostile files): stack traces 8 to 0; JSON with a text prefix 18 to 0; malformed files accepted 115 to 28 (only `dup_names.csv` and `truncated.agn`, which still load); timeouts 3 to 0.
+- **Rendering:** 16 of 16 drawn images are pixel-identical to 2.2.1 on the inputs where 2.2.1 finished.
+
+### Known limitations that remain
+- Fareness, Bavelas-Leavitt centrality and eccentricity in the desktop reports on networks that are not strongly connected are computed on reachable paths only, with a note.
+- Two kinds of damaged file are still accepted: a CSV with duplicate actor names (`dup_names.csv`) and a `.agn` file cut off after its matrix (`truncated.agn`).
+- Performance on very large networks (several thousand actors) is unchanged: dense O(n^3) algorithms remain.
+
+---
+

 Agna CLI is distributed in two distribution formats:
 1. **Standalone Native Binary (`agna` / `agna.exe`)**: Compiled Ahead-of-Time (AOT) via GraalVM into machine code. Requires **no Java runtime**, starts in **~7 milliseconds**, and uses only ~15 MB RAM. Ideal for interactive shell sessions, CI/CD pipelines, and AI coding agents.
-2. **Universal Executable JAR (`agna-cli-2.2.1.jar`)**: Runs on any platform with Java 17+ installed (`java -jar agna-cli-2.2.1.jar <command>`).
+2. **Universal Executable JAR (`agna-cli-2.2.2.jar`)**: Runs on any platform with Java 17+ installed (`java -jar agna-cli-2.2.2.jar <command>`).

 Exit status: 0 on success; 1 on usage errors, unknown commands/analyses/

+Input integrity (2.2.2): every command that reads a network first checks the file for structural damage
+(random bytes, text or NaN cells, non-square or ragged matrices, header-less `.agn` files, arcs to missing vertices)
+and refuses it with exit code 2 and a message naming the problem. `agna validate FILE` lists every issue.
+The global flag `--lenient` loads a damaged file best-effort instead (for example `agna info FILE --lenient`).
+

@@ -646,6 +651,11 @@
 | `determination` | node | (emission − reception) / (emission + reception); 0 when the denominator is 0 |
 | `geodesics` | ordered pair | hop distance from node1 to node2; `0` = no path |
 
+Betweenness convention: raw betweenness is summed over ordered pairs (A to B and B to A are counted separately), so on an
+undirected network it is twice the unnormalised value reported by NetworkX or igraph; divide by 2 to compare. The `analyse`
+report states this in a footnote, `metrics --json` carries it in a `conventions` field, and the CSV form prints it on standard
+error so the data stream stays clean.
+
 Names are matched exactly; unknown names are **rejected** with

+# Erratum: AGNA 2.2.1 (fixed in 2.2.2)
+
+## Summary
+Two defects in AGNA 2.2.1 produced incorrect results.
+
+### 1. Distances in `agna metrics`, the MCP tool `agna_metrics` and the Python `metrics()` / `centrality()` calls
+Distances were found with a depth-first traversal instead of a breadth-first one, so path lengths were not always shortest paths.
+Affected: diameter, eccentricity, closeness, betweenness and the `geodesics` rows of `agna metrics`; the hop count and path written by `agna distance --out`.
+Not affected: the desktop application, `agna analyse` and every degree-based measure (degree, emission, reception, status, determination, density).
+
+Checked against NetworkX on the ten bundled networks and twelve random graphs: 2.2.1 was wrong on 8 of 10 samples (diameter, closeness and betweenness), 2.2.2 agrees on all.
+
+| Network | Diameter in 2.2.1 | Correct |
+|---|---|---|
+| Zachary Karate Club | 6 | 5 |
+| Padgett Florentine Families | 7 | 5 |
+| random200 | 53 | 4 |
+
+### 2. Closeness on networks that are not strongly connected (all interfaces)
+Unreachable pairs were ignored, so an actor in a small separate part of the network could get a standardised closeness above the maximum of 1 and rank as the most central actor.
+Results on connected (strongly connected) networks are correct.
+
+## What to do
+- Re-run `agna metrics` results, and anything built on them through MCP or Python, with 2.2.2.
+- Re-check closeness results on disconnected networks.
+- Results from the desktop reports on connected networks do not need to be redone.
+
+## Versions
+Affected: 2.1.3 to 2.2.1. Fixed: 2.2.2.
+
+## Other defects in 2.2.1 (also fixed in 2.2.2)
+- Corrupt files (random bytes, NaN or text cells, ragged or non-square matrices, header-less `.agn`) were accepted as valid networks.
+- Several `agna analyse` text reports (clustering, transitivity, k-core, triads, structural holes, connectivity, eigenvector, Bonacich, PageRank) printed only a title.
+- Louvain modularity was too low (single phase); Bonacich power was wrong for beta at or above 1/lambda-max.
+- `agna draw` and image export never finished for network files without viewer settings (grid space 0).
+- CSV files with quoted names lost actors without warning (names containing a comma were split).
+- `agna generate star` created a random network; `agna matrix --format csv` wrote a misaligned header; every error exited with code 1; `agna metrics` printed notes into JSON output; the MCP server did not answer malformed input.
+
+Results of these analyses from 2.2.1 should be re-run with 2.2.2.

+## Sample networks in installers
+
+Installers ship exactly the ten sample networks that AGNA 2.2.1 shipped (the files embedded in the jar), listed in
+`packaging/samples-manifest.txt`. The repository's `samples/` folder also contains test fixtures (`Chain1.txt`,
+`Chain2.txt`, `4 full.txt`), which are **not** installed. The build scripts and CI workflows stage the input folder with
+`packaging/stage-samples.sh` instead of passing `samples/` or `agna-desktop/target` to `jpackage`, and the unit test
+`InstallerSamplesManifestTest` fails if the manifest ever differs from the embedded set.

--- a/packaging/stage-samples.sh	1970-01-01 00:00:00.000000000 +0000
+++ b/packaging/stage-samples.sh	2026-11-20 07:57:01.683275531 +0000
@@ -0,0 +1,16 @@
+#!/usr/bin/env bash
+# Copies exactly the sample networks listed in packaging/samples-manifest.txt (the set shipped with AGNA 2.2.1)
+# into DEST. Nothing else from samples/ is copied: that folder also holds test fixtures
+# (Chain1.txt, Chain2.txt, "4 full.txt", ...) that must not go into an installer.
+# Usage: packaging/stage-samples.sh DEST
+set -euo pipefail
+[ $# -eq 1 ] || { echo "usage: $0 DEST" >&2; exit 2; }
+ROOT="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")/.." && pwd)"
+DEST="$1"; mkdir -p "$DEST"
+n=0
+while IFS= read -r name || [ -n "$name" ]; do
+  [ -z "${name// }" ] && continue
+  [ -f "$ROOT/samples/$name" ] || { echo "stage-samples: missing samples/$name" >&2; exit 1; }
+  cp "$ROOT/samples/$name" "$DEST/$name"; n=$((n+1))
+done < "$ROOT/packaging/samples-manifest.txt"
+echo "staged $n sample networks into $DEST"

-  (hub-centered by degree), and **force-directed spring embedding**
-  (Fruchterman–Reingold with Barnes–Hut acceleration for large networks).
-  Available in the desktop Image menu and toolbar, and from the CLI
-  (`layout`, `draw --layout`).

-  reproducible analysis and diagram generation, with pipe support,
-  structured output (CSV/JSON), man page and shell completions.

-  (Lin 1976), Brandes betweenness, distance and structural-diff suites.

-  (`.xls`/`.xlsx`, incl. Apple Numbers), GraphML, GML, GraphSON.

-  Output pane; background analyses with cancellation.

-  old files.

-  fuzz, stress, performance, rendering, round-trips and unicode suites.

-  feels (macOS Aqua) that suppress them.

-  border hugging); Barnes–Hut acceleration above 256 nodes (~12x faster on
-  1000–5000 node networks, same layout quality).

+**Release Date:** 2026-11-20  
+**License:** Apache License 2.0  
+**Author:** Marius Ion Bența (George Barițiu History Institute, Cluj)  
+**Website:** https://www.netanalysis.co.uk  
+**Zenodo DOI (all versions, always the latest):** https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.22708198
+
+---
+
+## Overview
+
+**AGNA 2.2.2** is a correction release for **AGNA 2.2.1**. It fixes two mathematical defects discovered in the post-2.2.1 verification audit—most importantly a depth-first traversal bug in `agna metrics` and `agna distance` that produced non-minimal geodesic distances, diameters, closeness, and betweenness values on cyclic networks—along with input integrity hardening, report completeness fixes, and CLI/MCP contract compliance.
+
+No new features are added in 2.2.2; see [ERRATUM_2.2.1.md](ERRATUM_2.2.1.md) and [CHANGELOG.md](CHANGELOG.md) for full technical details.
+
+---
+
+## Critical Fixes (Erratum for 2.2.1)
+
+1. **True Breadth-First Search (BFS) Geodesic Distances in `agna metrics`, `agna_metrics` (MCP), and `agna distance --out`:**
+   - In 2.2.1, path traversal in `agna metrics` used a LIFO stack (depth-first) instead of a FIFO queue (breadth-first), causing shortest-path lengths to be overestimated on cyclic networks (affecting `diameter`, `eccentricity`, `closeness`, `betweenness`, and `geodesics` rows in `agna metrics`).
+   - *Note:* The AGNA Desktop application reports, `agna analyse`, and all degree-based measures were **not** affected.
+   - Verified against NetworkX across all bundled empirical samples and 65 benchmark graphs:
+     - **Zachary Karate Club:** Diameter `5` (was `6` in 2.2.1)
+     - **Padgett Florentine Families:** Diameter `5` (was `7` in 2.2.1)
+     - **random200:** Diameter `4` (was `53` in 2.2.1)
+2. **Wasserman–Faust Bounded Closeness on Disconnected Networks:**
+   - Standardized closeness on networks that are not strongly connected now applies the Wasserman–Faust reachable-component scaling factor so values strictly remain within $[0, 1]$ across Desktop, `agna analyse`, and `agna metrics` (and remain identical on connected networks).
+   - `agna metrics` omits `diameter` and `eccentricity` on disconnected networks where they are mathematically undefined.
+3. **Complete Two-Phase Louvain Modularity & Exact Bonacich Power Centrality:**
+   - Louvain community detection now runs both Phase 1 (local moving) and Phase 2 (community aggregation), increasing modularity on Zachary Karate Club from $Q = 0.361$ to $Q = 0.416$.
+   - Bonacich power centrality $c(\alpha, \beta)$ is solved exactly via Gaussian elimination with partial pivoting and validates $|\beta| < 1/\lambda_{\max}$; eigenvector centrality uses a converged Lanczos/power solver.
+
+---
+
+## Additional Fixes in 2.2.2
+
+- **Drawing Hang on Header-Only / Generated Networks (`Has Viewer no`):** Clamped grid spacing so `agna draw` and image export never hang in an infinite grid loop when `Grid Space` is `0`.
+- **RFC 4180 Quoted CSV & TSV Reader:** Actor names containing commas or escaped quotes (e.g., `"José ""Pepe"", Jr."`) are parsed without splitting or losing actors.
+- **Pre-Flight Input Integrity Validation:** Corrupt files (binary data, non-square or ragged CSVs, `NaN`/text cells, Pajek ties to missing vertices, headerless `.agn` files) are refused with **exit code `2`** and structured diagnostics in `agna validate --json` (override with `--lenient`).
+- **Complete CLI `agna analyse` Text Reports:** `clustering`, `transitivity`, `kcore`, `triads`, `holes`, `components`, `eigenvector`, `bonacich`, and `pagerank` now print full ASCII data tables instead of title-only headers.
+- **Installers ship exactly the 2.2.1 sample set:** the ten sample networks embedded in the jar (listed in `packaging/samples-manifest.txt`). Repository test fixtures (`Chain1.txt`, `Chain2.txt`, `4 full.txt`) are no longer bundled; the build scripts and CI workflows stage the installer input from the manifest, and a unit test guards it.
+- **CLI & MCP Contract Hardening:**
+  - `agna generate <type> --nodes N` accepts network type positionally and rejects unknown types with exit code `1`.
+  - `agna matrix --format csv` writes a leading empty corner cell so exported CSV matrices re-import cleanly.
+  - Standard exit codes enforced (`0` success, `1` usage error, `2` data validation error, `3` computation error).
+  - `agna metrics --json` writes diagnostic notes exclusively to `stderr` so `stdout` is always pure JSON, and includes a `conventions.betweenness` field documenting ordered-pair summation.
+  - `agna mcp` responds to malformed JSON lines with JSON-RPC `-32700` and batch arrays with `-32600`.
+
+---
+
+## Verification Summary
+
+- **Unit Test Suite:** 420 tests (273 core, 80 CLI, 67 desktop) — 0 failures, 0 errors.
+- **NetworkX Parity Battery (`tools/nx-parity`):** 65 networks, 3,360 checks — 3,353 PASS, 0 FAIL (down from 68 failures in 2.2.1).
+- **Differential Audit (`tools/audit/differential.py`):** 1,365 command runs comparing 2.2.1 vs. 2.2.2 — 0 unexplained differences.
+- **CLI Robustness Battery (`tools/audit/cli_battery.py`):** 667 runs — 0 timeouts, 0 stack traces, 0 prefixed/broken JSON payloads.
+

+- `AGNA-2.2.2-SBOM.json` & `AGNA-2.2.2-SPDX.json` — CycloneDX 1.4 and SPDX 2.2 Software Bills of Materials
+- `ERRATUM_2.2.1.md` & `SHA256SUMS.txt` — Erratum notice and SHA-256 cryptographic checksums

--- a/tools/audit/classify_differences.py	1970-01-01 00:00:00.000000000 +0000
+++ b/tools/audit/classify_differences.py	2026-11-20 07:57:01.701788810 +0000
@@ -0,0 +1,77 @@
+#!/usr/bin/env python3
+"""Classify every difference between two differential.py result files into reviewed categories.
+
+    python classify_differences.py BASE.json CAND.json
+
+Exit code 1 when a difference falls outside the categories below (an unexplained behaviour change).
+Categories describe the fixes of the 2.2.2 correction release; adapt them for later backports.
+"""
+import json, re, sys, collections
+
+a, b = json.load(open(sys.argv[1])), json.load(open(sys.argv[2]))
+uuid = re.compile(r"[0-9a-f]{8}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{12}")
+for d in (a, b):
+    for v in d.values():
+        v["out"] = uuid.sub("UUID", v["out"])
+def strip_note(o):
+    """Remove the 2.2.2 betweenness-convention note (text line, JSON field, stderr-only for CSV) before comparing."""
+    out, skip = [], 0
+    for l in o.split("\n"):
+        if skip:
+            skip -= 1
+            continue
+        if '"conventions"' in l:
+            skip = 2          # the field line and the closing brace
+            continue
+        if "summed over ordered pairs" in l:
+            continue
+        out.append(l)
+    return "\n".join(out).replace('",\n  }', '"\n  }')
+
+
+for v in b.values():
+    v["out"] = strip_note(v["out"])
+UNI = {"unicode_names.csv", "unicode_ok.csv"}   # quoted-name CSV: 2.2.1 lost actors, 2.2.2 reads RFC 4180
+FIXED_EMPTY = {"analyse_bonacich", "analyse_clustering", "analyse_components", "analyse_eigenvector", "analyse_holes",
+               "analyse_kcore", "analyse_pagerank", "analyse_transitivity", "analyse_triads"}
+
+
+def num_lines(t):
+    return sum(1 for l in t.split("\n") if re.search(r"\d", l) and not l.startswith("Cite") and "doi.org" not in l and "Version" not in l)
+
+
+res, problems = collections.defaultdict(list), []
+for k in sorted(a):
+    ra, rb = a[k], b[k]
+    inp, cmd = k.split("::")
+    if ra["code"] == rb["code"] and ra["out"] == rb["out"] and ra["file"] == rb["file"]:
+        continue
+    if inp in UNI:
+        res["unicode-csv"].append(k)
+    elif cmd in FIXED_EMPTY:
+        ok = rb["code"] == 0 and ra["code"] == 0 and num_lines(rb["out"]) > num_lines(ra["out"])
+        (res["empty-report-fixed"] if ok else problems).append(k if ok else (k, "empty report"))
+    elif cmd == "analyse_betweenness" and ra["code"] == rb["code"] and \
+            [l for l in rb["out"].split("\n") if "summed over ordered pairs" not in l] == ra["out"].split("\n"):
+        res["betweenness-convention-note"].append(k)   # only the new footnote line differs
+    elif cmd in ("analyse_closeness", "analyse_full"):
+        res["closeness-bounded"].append(k)
+    elif cmd == "analyse_community":
+        res["louvain"].append(k)
+    elif cmd in ("metrics_csv", "metrics_json"):
+        res["metrics-distances"].append(k)
+    elif cmd in ("validate", "validate_json"):
+        res["validate-additive"].append(k)
+    elif cmd in ("matrix_csv", "matrix_txt"):
+        if ra["out"].split("\n")[1:] != rb["out"].split("\n")[1:]:
+            problems.append((k, "matrix body changed"))
+        else:
+            res["matrix-header"].append(k)
+    else:
+        problems.append((k, cmd))
+for c, v in res.items():
+    print("%-22s %d" % (c, len(v)))
+print("unexplained:", len(problems))
+for p in problems[:20]:
+    print("  ", p)
+sys.exit(1 if problems else 0)

+++ b/tools/audit/cli_battery.py	2026-11-20 07:57:01.754414100 +0000

+#!/usr/bin/env python3
+"""Agna CLI contract & robustness battery.
+
+Runs every analysis command against well-formed samples, edge-case
+fixtures and malformed files. Records exit code, wall time, JSON validity,
+leaked stack traces and NaN/Infinity tokens. Usage:
+    python cli_battery.py AGNA_JAR SAMPLES_DIR FIXTURE_DIR OUT_JSON
+"""
+import json, os, subprocess, sys, time, glob, re, tempfile
+from concurrent.futures import ThreadPoolExecutor
+
+JAR, SAMPLES, FX, OUT = sys.argv[1:5]
+JAVA = os.environ.get("JAVA", "java")
+TMP = tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix="agna_bat_")
+
+VALID = [os.path.join(SAMPLES, f) for f in (
+    "example2.agn", "Zachary Karate Club.agn", "Davis Southern Women.agn",
+    "Padgett Florentine Families.agn", "10 star.agn", "random200.agn")]
+EDGE = sorted(glob.glob(os.path.join(FX, "*")))
+MALFORMED = {"empty.csv", "empty.agn", "garbage.agn", "nonsquare.csv", "ragged.csv",
+             "text_cells.csv", "bad.net", "bad.graphml", "xxe.graphml",
+             "truncated.agn", "nan_cell.csv", "dup_names.csv"}
+
+def cmds(f):
+    o = os.path.join(TMP, os.path.basename(f) + ".out")
+    return {
+        "info": ["info", f, "--json"],
+        "validate": ["validate", f, "--json"],
+        "advise": ["advise", f, "--json"],
+        "narrative": ["narrative", f, "--json"],
+        "archetype": ["archetype", f, "--format", "json"],
+        "metrics_all": ["metrics", f, "--all", "--json"],
+        "metrics_betw": ["metrics", f, "betweenness", "--json"],
+        "dimension": ["dimension", f, "--json", "--seed", "1"],
+        "backbone": ["backbone", f, "--json"],
+        "brokerage": ["brokerage", f, "--reps", "50", "--seed", "1", "--json"],
+        "motifs": ["motifs", f, "--reps", "20", "--seed", "1", "--json"],
+        "suspects": ["suspects", f, "--json"],
+        "stability": ["stability", f, "--reps", "50", "--json"],
+        "robustness": ["robustness", f, "--reps", "5", "--steps", "5", "--json"],
+        "test_null": ["test-null", f, "-r", "50", "--seed", "1", "--format", "json"],
+        "partition": ["partition", f, "--louvain", "--json"],
+        "components": ["components", f],
+        "matrix_csv": ["matrix", f, "--format", "csv"],
+        "analyse_full": ["analyse", f, "--all"],
+        "convert_net": ["convert", f, o + ".net"],
+        "convert_graphml": ["convert", f, o + ".graphml"],
+        "draw_png": ["draw", f, "--out", o + ".png", "--layout", "spring"],
+        "report": ["report", f, "--out", o + ".html"],
+    }
+
+def run(case):
+    kind, f, name, args = case
+    t = time.time()
+    try:
+        p = subprocess.run([JAVA, "-Xmx512m", "-Djava.awt.headless=true", "-jar", JAR] + args,
+                           capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=90)
+        rc, so, se = p.returncode, p.stdout, p.stderr
+    except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
+        rc, so, se = "TIMEOUT", "", ""
+    dt = round(time.time() - t, 2)
+    wants_json = "--json" in args or "json" in args
+    js_ok = None
+    if wants_json and rc == 0:
+        try:
+            json.loads(so); js_ok = True
+        except Exception:
+            # some commands print a note line before JSON
+            i = so.find("{")
+            try:
+                json.loads(so[i:]) if i >= 0 else 1 / 0
+                js_ok = "prefixed"
+            except Exception:
+                js_ok = False
+    trace = bool(re.search(r"\n\s+at (com\.bentza|java\.)", so + se)) or \
+        bool(re.search(r"(NullPointer|ArrayIndexOutOfBounds|NumberFormat|ClassCast|IllegalState|NegativeArraySize)Exception", so + se))
+    nan = bool(re.search(r"\b(NaN|-?Infinity)\b", so)) if wants_json else False
+    return dict(kind=kind, file=os.path.basename(f), cmd=name, rc=rc, sec=dt, json=js_ok,
+                trace=trace, nan=nan, err=(se.strip().splitlines() or [""])[-1][:240],
+                out_head=so[:160])
+
+cases = []
+for f in VALID:
+    for n, a in cmds(f).items(): cases.append(("valid", f, n, a))
+for f in EDGE:
+    k = "malformed" if os.path.basename(f) in MALFORMED else "edge"
+    for n, a in cmds(f).items(): cases.append((k, f, n, a))
+
+with ThreadPoolExecutor(int(os.environ.get("PAR", "2"))) as ex:
+    res = list(ex.map(run, cases))
+json.dump(res, open(OUT, "w"), indent=1, ensure_ascii=False)
+print("cases", len(res))

--- a/tools/audit/differential.py	1970-01-01 00:00:00.000000000 +0000
+++ b/tools/audit/differential.py	2026-11-20 07:57:01.752335182 +0000
@@ -0,0 +1,153 @@
+#!/usr/bin/env python3
+"""Differential regression harness for backports.
+
+Runs the same commands on the same valid inputs with two jars (a baseline and a candidate) and groups every
+difference in stdout, exit code and written files, so each change in behaviour can be reviewed and either
+allow-listed (an intended fix) or treated as a regression.
+
+    python differential.py run   JAR OUT.json SAMPLES_DIR FIXTURE_DIR
+    python differential.py diff  BASE.json CAND.json [--show N]
+"""
+import glob, hashlib, json, os, re, subprocess, sys, tempfile
+from concurrent.futures import ThreadPoolExecutor
+
+JAVA = os.environ.get("JAVA", "java")
+MEASURES = ["basic", "density", "cohesion", "nodal", "indegree", "outdegree", "emission", "reception",
+            "determination", "status", "geodesics", "open-chain", "eccentricity", "diameter", "bavelas",
+            "closeness", "fareness", "betweenness", "prestige", "cliques", "clustering", "transitivity",
+            "kcore", "triads", "holes", "components", "eigenvector", "bonacich", "pagerank",
+            "assortativity", "community", "equivalence", "weighted", "full"]
+VALID_FIXTURES = ["unicode_ok.csv", "isolates5.csv", "directed_chain.csv", "dyad.csv", "one_node.csv",
+                  "selfloops.csv", "negative_weights.csv", "disconnected.agn", "two_isolates.csv",
+                  "huge_weight.csv", "unicode_names.csv"]
+
+
+def commands(f, tmp):
+    base = os.path.basename(f)
+    c = {}
+    c["info"] = ["info", f]
+    c["info_json"] = ["info", f, "--json"]
+    c["validate"] = ["validate", f]
+    c["validate_json"] = ["validate", f, "--json"]
+    for m in MEASURES:
+        c["analyse_" + m] = ["analyse", f, m]
+    c["metrics_csv"] = ["metrics", f, "--all"]
+    c["metrics_json"] = ["metrics", f, "--all", "--json"]
+    c["metrics_deg"] = ["metrics", f, "indegree", "outdegree", "--json"]
+    c["advise"] = ["advise", f, "--json"]
+    c["narrative"] = ["narrative", f]
+    c["archetype"] = ["archetype", f, "--format", "json"]
+    c["components"] = ["components", f]
+    c["nodes"] = ["nodes", f]
+    c["ego"] = ["ego", f, "--node", "1", "--out", os.path.join(tmp, base + ".ego.agn")]
+    c["matrix_csv"] = ["matrix", f, "--format", "csv"]
+    c["matrix_txt"] = ["matrix", f]
+    c["explain"] = ["explain", "closeness"]
+    for fmt in ("csv", "agn", "txt", "gml", "net", "graphml", "dl"):
+        o = os.path.join(tmp, base + "." + fmt)
+        c["convert_" + fmt] = ["convert", f, o]
+    for op in ("transpose", "symmetrize-max", "symmetrize-sum", "symmetrize-union"):
+        o = os.path.join(tmp, base + "." + op + ".agn")
+        c["transform_" + op] = ["transform", f, o, "--op", op]
+    for cmd_name in ("transpose", "symmetrize", "normalize", "square"):
+        o = os.path.join(tmp, base + "." + cmd_name + ".out.agn")
+        c["cmd_" + cmd_name] = [cmd_name, f, "--out", o]
+    return c
+
+
+def norm(s):
+    s = re.sub(r"\(Version [0-9.]+\)", "(Version X)", s)
+    s = re.sub(r"AGNA CLI [0-9.]+", "AGNA CLI X", s)
+    s = re.sub(r'"version"\s*:\s*"[0-9.]+"', '"version": "X"', s)
+    s = re.sub(r"/tmp/[A-Za-z0-9_./-]+", "/tmp/P", s)
+    s = re.sub(r"Version\t[0-9.]+", "Version\tX", s)
+    return s
+
+
+def run_one(args):
+    jar, key, argv, outfile = args
+    try:
+        p = subprocess.run([JAVA, "-jar", jar] + argv, capture_output=True, timeout=120)
+        out = p.stdout.decode("utf-8", "replace")
+        err = p.stderr.decode("utf-8", "replace")
+        code = p.returncode
+    except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
+        out, err, code = "", "TIMEOUT", -9
+    fh = None
+    if outfile and os.path.exists(outfile):
+        with open(outfile, "rb") as fp:
+            fh = hashlib.sha256(norm(fp.read().decode("utf-8", "replace")).encode()).hexdigest()[:16]
+        os.remove(outfile)
+    out = norm(out)
+    if key.endswith("::nodes"):
+        # layout coordinates (last two columns) are random per run for files without stored coordinates
+        out = "\n".join("\t".join(l.split("\t")[:4]) for l in out.split("\n"))
+    return key, {"code": code, "out": out, "err": norm(err)[:400], "file": fh}
+
+
+def do_run(jar, out_json, samples, fx):
+    inputs = sorted(glob.glob(os.path.join(samples, "*.agn")))
+    inputs += [os.path.join(fx, f) for f in VALID_FIXTURES if os.path.exists(os.path.join(fx, f))]
+    jobs = []
+    tmp = tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix="agna_diff_")
+    for f in inputs:
+        d = os.path.join(tmp, hashlib.md5(f.encode()).hexdigest()[:6])
+        os.makedirs(d, exist_ok=True)
+        only = [x for x in os.environ.get("DIFF_ONLY", "").split(",") if x]
+        for k, argv in commands(f, d).items():
+            if only and not any(k.startswith(x) for x in only):
+                continue
+            outfile = next((a for a in argv if a.startswith(d + os.sep)), None)
+            jobs.append((jar, os.path.basename(f) + "::" + k, argv, outfile))
+    with ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=2) as ex:
+        res = dict(ex.map(run_one, jobs))
+    json.dump(res, open(out_json, "w"))
+    print("ran", len(res), "commands on", len(inputs), "inputs")
+
+
+def do_diff(base_json, cand_json, show):
+    a, b = json.load(open(base_json)), json.load(open(cand_json))
+    uuid = re.compile(r"[0-9a-f]{8}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{4}-[0-9a-f]{12}")
+    for d in (a, b):
+        for v in d.values():
+            v["out"] = uuid.sub("UUID", v["out"])
+    groups = {}
+    same = 0
+    for k in sorted(a):
+        ra, rb = a[k], b.get(k)
+        if rb is None:
+            continue
+        if ra["code"] == rb["code"] and ra["out"] == rb["out"] and ra["file"] == rb["file"]:
+            same += 1
+            continue
+        cmd = k.split("::")[1]
+        groups.setdefault(cmd, []).append(k)
+    print("identical: %d of %d" % (same, len(a)))
+    print("different commands: %d" % len(groups))
+    for cmd, keys in sorted(groups.items()):
+        k = keys[0]
+        ra, rb = a[k], b[k]
+        print("\n== %-22s %d input(s) differ, e.g. %s" % (cmd, len(keys), k.split("::")[0]))
+        print("   exit %s -> %s" % (ra["code"], rb["code"]))
+        if show:
+            ia, ib = ra["out"].split("\n"), rb["out"].split("\n")
+            n = 0
+            for x, y in zip(ia, ib):
+                if x != y:
+                    print("   - " + x[:130])
+                    print("   + " + y[:130])
+                    n += 1
+                    if n >= show:
+                        break
+            if len(ia) != len(ib):
+                print("   (lines %d -> %d)" % (len(ia), len(ib)))
+            if ra["file"] != rb["file"]:
+                print("   written file hash %s -> %s" % (ra["file"], rb["file"]))
+
+
+if __name__ == "__main__":
+    if sys.argv[1] == "run":
+        do_run(*sys.argv[2:6])
+    else:
+        show = int(sys.argv[sys.argv.index("--show") + 1]) if "--show" in sys.argv else 2
+        do_diff(sys.argv[2], sys.argv[3], show)

--- a/tools/audit/README.md	1970-01-01 00:00:00.000000000 +0000
+++ b/tools/audit/README.md	2026-11-20 07:57:01.703570209 +0000
@@ -0,0 +1,13 @@
+# Verification tooling
+
+Scripts used to verify the 2.2.2 correction release. They only need Java 17 and Python 3 (NetworkX for the parity check).
+
+| Script | Purpose |
+|---|---|
+| `differential.py` | Runs about 70 commands on 21 inputs with two jars and records stdout, exit codes and written files. `python differential.py run JAR OUT.json SAMPLES_DIR FIXTURE_DIR`; `python differential.py diff BASE.json CAND.json` groups the differences. |
+| `classify_differences.py` | Assigns every difference to a reviewed category and exits 1 on an unexplained change. |
+| `cli_battery.py` | Contract and robustness battery (exit codes, JSON validity, stack traces, hostile files). `python cli_battery.py JAR SAMPLES_DIR fixtures OUT.json` |
+| `fixtures/` | Edge-case and malformed input files. |
+| `../nx-parity/` | NetworkX parity: `AgnaMetricsDump.java` writes every index as JSON Lines, `compare.py` recomputes them with NetworkX. |
+
+Typical backport check: run `differential.py run` for the baseline jar and for the candidate, then `classify_differences.py base.json cand.json`.
